# CloudTCP IF v2.0.0 rule and gap traceability

Implementation status: active data-contract, standalone validation and producer work is delivered. See [implemented interfaces and limits](implementation.md) and [release-gate evidence](release-checklist.md). Verifier behavior remains deferred; source-policy and engineering dependencies in this chapter remain unresolved.

This chapter preserves the audit inventory while mapping candidate missing facts to the proposed v2 contract. It is an implementation planning map, not a claim that schema expansion executes a rule or resolves its legal interpretation. Baseline: verifier `ef5e47459aea9559ae8fad7f0087f376308fe09e`.

Active scope preserves this fact-to-schema mapping and source inventory. All implementation/evaluator, source-policy and judgment dependencies in the tables remain unresolved follow-up items, not active schema release gates. Earliest data stages R0–R3 refer only to the revised standalone validation and producer roadmap.

## Coverage and counting

All 37 audit families appear below. The following clause inventory contains 504 unique source-clause rows across 457 headings and 419 canonical checks; informational clauses have no canonical violation check. Full-document rows: 384; W1 rows: 120. Overlapping aliases remain separate source rows but must not create duplicate findings.

Confidence is inherited from the audit: 229 rows high, 275 medium. Static candidate assignments require clause-specific review before implementing a prerequisite manifest. The stage is the earliest planned data capability, not a promise that all its evaluators are delivered then. G37 is a cross-cutting evidence concern without a fabricated source-clause requirement.

An OPTION permits or conditions a selected treatment; SUPPORT explains context. STANDARD and GUIDANCE retain their actual clause strength. No table row makes an optional treatment mandatory.

## Reading a source-clause row

The required facts and residual blockers are the audit’s clause-specific findings. Proposed bindings route those facts to v2 structures; they are not blanket assertions that every listed property is needed for every subcase. Read the cited source and preserve supported existing-input subcases. An unresolved external criterion or human decision remains a blocker even after its input objects exist.

Examples: T-001 taper placement maps to taper/zone links without new mandatory placement; B-004 buffer permission maps to optional buffer/work scope; SIGN-24.c01 content maps to sign code/legend; fixed SIGN-31 sizes can use supplied dimensions before roadway-tier logic is available. TAPER-01 alternatives need qualified speed evidence, while its posted-speed subset is already expressible; 40<S<45 remains policy.

The source URLs pin the original clauses. Detailed original evidence and normative notes remain in the [audit matrix](../../reports/schema-gap-audit/rule-gap-matrix.csv).

## Deferred prerequisite manifest authoring

Verifier manifests and rule activation are deferred and do not gate schema or producer releases. For the separately authorized future verifier workstream, a candidate manifest would name: source aliases and strength; subject kind and phase; applicable jurisdiction/layout; exact field paths and target-kind relationships; accepted units and derivations; evidence/complete-inventory prerequisites; permitted speed bases; resolved policy versions; judgment conditions; evaluator capability/version; and acceptance scenarios. Missing entries keep the check unsupported or unready. This is provisional follow-up guidance, not active implementation work or an executable manifest in this package.

Use the original source to resolve medium-confidence family assignments before freezing a field contract. Correcting a candidate mapping must preserve the source clause and record why its needed facts changed. Do not delete difficult rows or count aliases as newly implemented checks.
